\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\nabla^{T}\mathcal{L}_{i}K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})\nabla^{T}\mathcal{L}_{i}K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})^{T}K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}].

(E.15)

For the last two terms, we apply the tower property of conditional expectation by first conditioning on the randomness of
\{S_{i,j}\}_{j}
to take expectation over the randomness of
\xi_{i}
, and then taking expectation over the randomness of
\{S_{i,j}\}_{j}
. In particular, we have (similar for the second last term in (
E.4.1
))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})^{T}K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\mathbb{E}[{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\cup\sigma(\{S_{i,j}\}_{j})]^{T}K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\mathbb{E}[{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]^{T}K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]={\bm{0}}.

For the second term in (
E.4.1
), it converges to zero almost surely as
i\rightarrow\infty
since
\|\widetilde{C}_{i}\|\vee\|C_{i}\|\leq 1
,
\|K_{i}^{-1}\|\leq\Upsilon_{K}
, and
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\rightarrow 0
. For the first term in (
E.4.1
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})^{T}K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})^{T}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\cup\sigma(\{S_{i,j}\}_{j})\right]K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})^{T}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]K_{i}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{i}^{T}-C_{i}^{T})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:tC}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;\mathbb{E}[(\widetilde{C}^{\star}-C^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}((\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}-C^{\star})]=\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]-C^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}C^{\star}.

Again, the convergence here is due to the dominated convergence theorem, (
E.13
), and
K_{i}\rightarrow K^{\star}
; and the expectation is taken over the randomness of
\tau
sketch matrices
S_{1},\ldots,S_{\tau}
only. Thus, combining the above two displays with (
E.4.1
), we have

\mathcal{J}_{2,i}=\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]-C^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}C^{\star}+O(\mathcal{K}_{2,i})

(E.16)

with
\mathcal{K}_{2,i}\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
almost surely. Combining (
E.16
), (
E.14
), and (
E.4.1
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}[\bm{\theta}^{i}(\bm{\theta}^{i})\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]=\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]+O(\mathcal{K}_{1,i}+\mathcal{K}_{2,i}).

By the definition of
\langle\mathcal{I}_{1}\rangle_{t}
in (
E.10
), let us denote
\Gamma\coloneqq U^{T}\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]U
. For any
k,l\in\{1,\ldots,d+m\}
, the
(k,l)
entry of the matrix
U^{T}\langle\mathcal{I}_{1}\rangle_{t}U
can be written as

[U^{T}\langle\mathcal{I}_{1}\rangle_{t}U]_{k,l}=\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{l})\varphi_{i}^{2}(\Gamma_{kl}+r_{i,kl}),

where
r_{i,kl}\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
almost surely. By Lemma
4.4
(b) and the fact that
C_{i}\rightarrow C^{\star}
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, we know
\|C^{\star}\|\leq\rho^{\tau}
. Since
C^{\star}\preceq{\bm{0}}
, we have
0<1-\rho^{\tau}\leq\sigma_{i}\leq 1
for
i=1,\ldots,d+m
, which implies
\sigma_{k}+\sigma_{l}\geq 2(1-\rho^{\tau})
. Using the condition (
E.6
), Lemmas
B.2
and
B.3
, we obtain
[U^{T}\langle\mathcal{I}_{1}\rangle_{t}U]_{k,l}/\varphi_{t}\rightarrow\Gamma_{kl}/(\sigma_{k}+\sigma_{l}+\varphi/\widetilde{\varphi})
as
t\rightarrow\infty
almost surely. Thus, by (
5.11
), we have

\langle\mathcal{I}_{1}\rangle_{t}/\varphi_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a.s.}}{{\longrightarrow}}U(\Theta\circ\Gamma)U^{T}=\Xi^{\star}.

(E.17)

Then,
(
Duflo 1997
, Theorem 1.3.15)
indicates (
E.7
) holds. This shows the first part of the results. For the second part of the results, we assume the condition
(
4.2b
)
and have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\theta}^{i}\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\;\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{rec:def:b}}}}{{\leq}}\;\;4\left(\mathbb{E}[\|(I+C_{i})K_{i}^{-1}({\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i})\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}-(I+C_{i}){\widetilde{\bm{z}}}_{i}\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:z:recur}}}}{{\leq}}4\left(8\Upsilon_{K}^{3}\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{g}}_{i}-\nabla f_{i}\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]+\mathbb{E}[\|(\widetilde{C}_{i}-C_{i}){\widetilde{\bm{z}}}_{i}\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right)\quad(\|C_{i}\|\leq 1,\|K_{i}^{-1}\|\leq\Upsilon_{K})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:BM:b}}}}{{\leq}}\;4\left(8\Upsilon_{K}^{3}\Upsilon_{m}+8\mathbb{E}[\|{\widetilde{\bm{z}}}_{i}\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right)\quad(\|\widetilde{C}_{i}\|\vee\|C_{i}\|\leq 1)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:Newton}}}}{{\leq}}4\left(8\Upsilon_{K}^{3}\Upsilon_{m}+8\Upsilon_{K}^{3}\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{\nabla}}\mathcal{L}_{i}\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right)\quad(\|K_{i}^{-1}\|\leq\Upsilon_{K})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:Newton}}}}{{\leq}}4\left(8\Upsilon_{K}^{3}\Upsilon_{m}+8\Upsilon_{K}^{3}\left\{4\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{i}\|^{3}+4\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{g}}_{i}-\nabla f_{i}\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:BM:b}}}}{{\leq}}4\left(8\Upsilon_{K}^{3}\Upsilon_{m}+8\Upsilon_{K}^{3}\left\{4\Upsilon_{u}^{3}+4\Upsilon_{m}\right\}\right)\quad(\text{also use }\eqref{equ:upper:bound}).

(E.18)

Thus,
\bm{\theta}^{i}
has bounded third moment; and
(
Wang 1995
, pp. 554)
together with (
E.17
) give the result (a). For (b), we verify the Lindeberg’s condition. For any
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varphi_{t}}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big[\big\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left\{I-\varphi_{j}(I+C^{\star})\right\}\varphi_{i}\bm{\theta}^{i}\big\|^{2}\cdot{\bm{1}}_{\left\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left\{I-\varphi_{j}(I+C^{\star})\right\}\varphi_{i}\bm{\theta}^{i}\right\|\geq\epsilon\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon\varphi_{t}^{3/2}}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big[\big\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left\{I-\varphi_{j}(I+C^{\star})\right\}\varphi_{i}\bm{\theta}^{i}\big\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon\varphi_{t}^{3/2}}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\big[\big\|\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left\{I-\varphi_{j}\Sigma\right\}\varphi_{i}U^{T}\bm{\theta}^{i}\big\|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\big].

To show the right hand side converges to zero, it suffices to show that each entry of the vector on the right hand side converges to zero. In particular, we show for any
1\leq k\leq d+m
,

\frac{1}{\epsilon\varphi_{t}^{3/2}}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left|1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}\right|^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}\mathbb{E}[|[U^{T}\bm{\theta}^{i}]_{k}|^{3}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\longrightarrow 0\quad\quad\text{ as }\quad t\rightarrow\infty.

By (
E.4.1
) and
\mathbb{E}[|[U^{T}\bm{\theta}^{i}]_{k}|^{3}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\theta}^{i}\|^{3}|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]
, we only show
\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}|1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}|^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}=o(\varphi_{t}^{3/2})
. Without loss of generality, we suppose
1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}\geq 0
for all
j\geq 1
and show

\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}=o(\varphi_{t}^{3/2}).

(E.19)

Otherwise, since
\varphi<0
from (
E.6
), Lemma
B.2
shows that
\varphi_{i}\rightarrow 0
. Thus, there exists
\widetilde{t}
such that
1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}\geq 0
,
\forall j\geq\widetilde{t}
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left|1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}\right|^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\widetilde{t}-2}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left|1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}\right|^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}+\sum_{i=\widetilde{t}-1}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=\widetilde{t}}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})^{3}\sum_{i=0}^{\widetilde{t}-2}\prod_{j=i+1}^{\widetilde{t}-1}\left|1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}\right|^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}+\sum_{i=\widetilde{t}-1}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=\widetilde{t}-1}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})^{3}(\varphi_{i}^{\prime})^{3},

(E.20)

where

\varphi_{\widetilde{t}-1}^{\prime}=\left(\sum_{i=0}^{\widetilde{t}-2}\prod_{j=i+1}^{\widetilde{t}-1}\left|1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}\right|^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}+\varphi_{\widetilde{t}-1}^{3}\right)^{1/3},\quad\text{ and }\quad\varphi_{i}^{\prime}=\varphi_{i},\quad\forall i\geq\widetilde{t}.

Note that (
E.4.1
) has the same form as (
E.19
), and
\varphi_{i}^{\prime}
differs from
\varphi_{i}
only at
i=\widetilde{t}-1
. Thus, (
E.4.1
) and (
E.19
) have the same limit. For (
E.19
), we apply Lemma
B.1
and observe that

\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\varphi_{i-1}^{2}/\varphi_{i}^{2}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{cond:varphi}}}{{=}}2\varphi\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad 3\sigma_{k}+2\varphi/\widetilde{\varphi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{cond:varphi}}}{{>}}0.

Thus, Lemma
B.3
suggests that

\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})^{3}\varphi_{i}^{3}=O(\varphi_{t}^{2}).

This verifies (
E.19
) and further verifies the Lindeberg’s condition. Thus, the central limit theorem of martingale in
(
Duflo 1997
, Corollary 2.1.10)
leads to (b). For (c), we apply
(
Fan 2019
, Theorem 2.1)
with
\epsilon=\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}
,
\delta=0
,
\rho=1
(in their notation), as proved for
verifying
the Lindeberg’s condition above, and obtain the result immediately. This completes the proof.

E.4.2
Proof of Lemma
E.2

We have

\mathcal{I}_{2,t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{rec:b}}}{{=}}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left\{I-\varphi_{j}(I+C^{\star})\right\}({\bar{\alpha}}_{i}-\varphi_{i}){\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}=U\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left\{I-\varphi_{j}\Sigma\right\}({\bar{\alpha}}_{i}-\varphi_{i})U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}.

(E.21)

Thus, for any
1\leq k\leq d+m
, we have
[U^{T}\mathcal{I}_{2,t}]_{k}=\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})({\bar{\alpha}}_{i}-\varphi_{i})[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}
. For the same reason as (
E.19
) and (
E.4.1
), we suppose for any
j\geq 0
that
1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}\geq 0
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|[U^{T}\mathcal{I}_{2,t}]_{k}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}|1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k}|\chi_{i}\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})\chi_{i}\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})\chi_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})\chi_{i}\big\{\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\left[\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\big\}\eqqcolon\mathcal{J}_{3,t,k}+\mathcal{J}_{4,t,k}.

(E.22)

We analyze
\mathcal{J}_{3,t,k}
and
\mathcal{J}_{4,t,k}
separately as follows. We first show
\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|
has bounded variance. We have

\mathbb{E}\big[\left\{\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|-\mathbb{E}\left[\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\right\}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\big]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pequ:7}}}{{\leq}}16\Upsilon_{K}^{2}(\Upsilon_{u}^{2}\vee\Upsilon_{m}).

(E.23)

Thus,
\mathcal{J}_{4,t,k}
is square integrable. Its variance is bounded by

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{J}_{4,k}\rangle_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{4}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})^{2}\chi_{i}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|-\mathbb{E}\left[\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\right\}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{pequ:9}}}}{{\leq}}\;\;4\Upsilon_{K}^{2}(\Upsilon_{u}^{2}\vee\Upsilon_{m})\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})^{2}\chi_{i}^{2}.

Using (
E.6
) and (
E.8
), we know

\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\chi_{i-1}^{2}/\varphi_{i-1}}{\chi_{i}^{2}/\varphi_{i}}\right)=\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\chi_{i-1}^{2}}{\chi_{i}^{2}}+\frac{\chi_{i-1}^{2}}{\chi_{i}^{2}}\left(1-\frac{\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i-1}}\right)\right)\\
=\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left\{\left(1-\frac{\chi_{i-1}}{\chi_{i}}\right)\left(1+\frac{\chi_{i-1}}{\chi_{i}}\right)-\frac{\chi_{i-1}^{2}}{\chi_{i}^{2}}\frac{\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i-1}}\left(1-\frac{\varphi_{i-1}}{\varphi_{i}}\right)\right\}=2\chi-\varphi.

(E.24)

Further, (
E.8
) implies
2\sigma_{k}+p(2\chi-\varphi)/\widetilde{\varphi}>0
for some constant
p\in(0,1]
. Thus, Lemma
B.3
leads to
\langle\mathcal{J}_{4,k}\rangle_{t}=O(\chi_{t}^{2p}/\varphi_{t}^{p})
(when
p\in(0,1)
,
O(\cdot)
can be strengthened to
o(\cdot)
); and the strong law of large number
(
Duflo 1997
, Theorem 1.3.15)
suggests that for any
\nu>0
,

\mathcal{J}_{4,t,k}=o\left(\sqrt{\chi_{t}^{2p}/\varphi_{t}^{p}\cdot\{\log(\varphi_{t}^{p}/\chi_{t}^{2p})\}^{1+\nu}}\right)=o\left(\sqrt{\chi_{t}^{2p}/\varphi_{t}^{p}\cdot\{\log(1/\chi_{t})\}^{1+\nu}}\right).

(E.25)

If (
4.2a
) is strengthened to (
4.2b
), then we follow (
E.23
), (
D.3
), and (
E.4.1
), and can show
\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|-\mathbb{E}\left[\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]
has bounded third moment. Thus,
(
Wang 1995
, pp. 554)
suggests that
\mathcal{J}_{4,t,k}=O(\chi_{t}^{p}/\varphi_{t}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\log(1/\chi_{t})})
. When
p\in(0,1)
,
O(\cdot)
can be strengthened to
o(\cdot)
due to
\langle\mathcal{J}_{4,k}\rangle_{t}=o(\chi_{t}^{2p}/\varphi_{t}^{p})
. For the term
\mathcal{J}_{3,t,k}
, we have

\mathcal{J}_{3,t,k}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})\chi_{i}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\left|[U^{T}{\bm{z}}_{i,\tau}]_{k}\right|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]}\;\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{pequ:9}}}}{{\leq}}\;\;2\Upsilon_{K}(\Upsilon_{u}\vee\sqrt{\Upsilon_{m}})\sum_{i=0}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(1-\varphi_{j}\sigma_{k})\chi_{i}.

(E.26)

Using (
E.6
), (
E.8
), and the facts that
\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i(1-\frac{\chi_{i-1}/\varphi_{i-1}}{\chi_{i}/\varphi_{i}})=\chi-\varphi
and
\sigma_{k}+q(\chi-\varphi)/\widetilde{\varphi}>0
(as implied by (
E.8
)), we apply Lemma
B.3
and obtain
\mathcal{J}_{3,t,k}=O(\chi_{t}^{q}/\varphi_{t}^{q})
. When
q\in(0,1)
,
O(\cdot)
can be strengthened to
o(\cdot)
. Combining with (
E.4.2
) and the bound of
\mathcal{J}_{4,t,k}
, we complete the proof.

E.4.3
Proof of Lemma
E.3

Based on the definition of
\mathcal{I}_{3,t}
in (
5.2c
), we have the recursion

\mathcal{I}_{3,t+1}=\left\{I-\varphi_{t+1}(I+C^{\star})\right\}\mathcal{I}_{3,t}+\varphi_{t+1}\bm{\delta}^{t+1}.

(E.27)

By Assumption
4.1
and the fact that
\|C_{t}\|\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\left\|\bm{\delta}^{t}\right\|\;\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{rec:def:c}}}}{{\leq}}\;\;2\left(\|(K^{\star})^{-1}\|\|\bm{\psi}^{t}\|+\|K_{t}^{-1}-(K^{\star})^{-1}\|\cdot\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{t}\|\right)+\|C_{t}-C^{\star}\|\cdot\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\Upsilon_{K}\Upsilon_{L}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|^{2}+(2\Upsilon_{K}^{2}\Upsilon_{u}\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|+\|C_{t}-C^{\star}\|)\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|.

(E.28)

Since
K_{t}\rightarrow K^{\star}
(cf. Lemma
E.4
) and
C_{t}\rightarrow C^{\star}
, we know

\bm{\delta}^{t}=o(\|({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})\|).

(E.29)

Using
\|C^{\star}\|\leq\rho^{\tau}
, we know for any
a\in(0,1)
, there exists an integer
t_{1}
such that for any
t\geq\penalty\ t_{1}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{I}_{3,t+1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\{1-\varphi_{t+1}(1-\rho^{\tau})\right\}\|\mathcal{I}_{3,t}\|+\varphi_{t+1}\cdot o\left(\left\|({\bm{x}}_{t+1}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t+1}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\{1-\varphi_{t+1}(1-\rho^{\tau})+o(\varphi_{t+1})\right\}\|\mathcal{I}_{3,t}\|+\varphi_{t+1}\cdot o(\|\mathcal{I}_{1,t}\|+\|\mathcal{I}_{2,t}\|)\quad(\text{by Lemma \ref{lem:4}})

\displaystyle\leq\left\{1-a(1-\rho^{\tau})\varphi_{t+1}\right\}\|\mathcal{I}_{3,t}\|+\varphi_{t+1}\cdot o(\|\mathcal{I}_{1,t}\|+\|\mathcal{I}_{2,t}\|).

We apply the above inequality recursively and obtain

\|\mathcal{I}_{3,t+1}\|\leq\prod_{j=t_{1}+1}^{t+1}\left\{1-a(1-\rho^{\tau})\varphi_{j}\right\}\|\mathcal{I}_{3,t_{1}}\|\\
+\sum_{i=t_{1}+1}^{t+1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t+1}\left\{1-a(1-\rho^{\tau})\varphi_{j}\right\}\varphi_{i}o(\|\mathcal{I}_{1,i-1}\|+\|\mathcal{I}_{2,i-1}\|).

(E.30)

We apply Lemmas
E.1
and
E.2
for bounding
\|\mathcal{I}_{1,i-1}\|
and
\|\mathcal{I}_{2,i-1}\|
. In particular, we note that for any
\upsilon\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\sqrt{\varphi_{i-1}\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{i-1})\right\}^{1+\upsilon}}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{i}\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{i})\right\}^{1+\upsilon}}}\right)\;\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{cond:varphi}}}}{{=}}\;\;\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\sqrt{\varphi_{i-1}}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{i}}}\right)+\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{i-1})\right\}^{\frac{1+\upsilon}{2}}}{\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{i})\right\}^{\frac{1+\upsilon}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{cond:varphi}}}}{{=}}\;\;\frac{\varphi}{2}+\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{i-1})\right\}^{\frac{1+\upsilon}{2}}}{\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{i})\right\}^{\frac{1+\upsilon}{2}}}\right)\quad(\text{Lemma \ref{aux:lem:5}}).

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\log(1/\varphi_{i-1})}{\log(1/\varphi_{i})}\right)

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}\frac{i\log(\varphi_{i-1}/\varphi_{i})}{\log(1/\varphi_{i})}=\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}\frac{i\log\left(1+(\varphi_{i-1}-\varphi_{i})/\varphi_{i}\right)}{\log(1/\varphi_{i})}

\displaystyle=\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}\frac{i\left\{\frac{\varphi_{i-1}-\varphi_{i}}{\varphi_{i}}+O\left(\frac{(\varphi_{i-1}-\varphi_{i})^{2}}{\varphi_{i}^{2}}\right)\right\}}{\log(1/\varphi_{i})}=\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}\frac{-\varphi}{\log(1/\varphi_{i})}=0,

where the last equality is due to
\varphi_{i}\rightarrow 0
, as implied by Lemma
B.2
. Combining the above two displays with Lemma
B.1
, we have

\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\sqrt{\varphi_{i-1}\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{i-1})\right\}^{1+\upsilon}}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{i}\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{i})\right\}^{1+\upsilon}}}\right)=\frac{\varphi}{2}\quad\quad\text{for any }\;\;\nu\geq 0.

(E.31)

Moreover, we have for any
p,q\in(0,1]
and
\nu\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\chi_{i-1}^{p}/\varphi_{i-1}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\{\log(1/\chi_{i-1})\}^{1+\nu}}}{\chi_{i}^{p}/\varphi_{i}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\{\log(1/\chi_{i})\}^{1+\nu}}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{pequ:12}\\
\eqref{com:chi:phi}\end{subarray}}}{{=}}p(\chi-0.5\varphi),

(E.32)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}i\left(1-\frac{\chi_{i-1}^{q}/\varphi_{i-1}^{q}}{\chi_{i}^{q}/\varphi_{i}^{q}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\begin{subarray}{c}\eqref{com:chi:phi}\end{subarray}}}{{=}}q(\chi-\varphi).

For the constants
p,q
in (
E.8
), we let
a
be any scalar such that

0<\frac{-\varphi/\widetilde{\varphi}}{2(1-\rho^{\tau})}\vee\frac{-p(\chi-0.5\varphi)/\widetilde{\varphi}}{1-\rho^{\tau}}\vee\frac{-q(\chi-\varphi)/\widetilde{\varphi}}{1-\rho^{\tau}}<a<1,

which is guaranteed to exist due to (
E.6
) and (
E.8
). Then, we obtain

a(1-\rho^{\tau})+\frac{\varphi}{2\widetilde{\varphi}}>0\quad a(1-\rho^{\tau})+\frac{p(\chi-0.5\varphi)}{\widetilde{\varphi}}>0\quad\text{and}\quad a(1-\rho^{\tau})+\frac{q(\chi-\varphi)}{\widetilde{\varphi}}>0.

(E.33)

Thus, combining (
E.30
), (
E.31
), and (
E.32
) with Lemma
B.3
, we obtain the results
under

either
(
4.2a
) or (
4.2b
). This completes the proof.

E.4.4
Proof of Lemma
E.4

We note that

\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{H}}_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\right\|+\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}\right\|\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{Lip:mL}}}}{{\leq}}\;\;\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{H}}_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\right\|+\frac{\Upsilon_{L}}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|.

(E.34)

Since
({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})\rightarrow{\bm{0}}
, by the fact that
a_{t}\rightarrow a
implies
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}a_{i}\rightarrow a
(also known as Stolz–Cesàro theorem), it suffices to show
(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{H}}_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i})/t
converges to zero. In fact, by Assumption
4.2
(
4.2d
) that
\mathbb{E}[{\bar{H}}_{i}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]=\nabla^{2}f_{i}
and
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bar{H}}_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\leq\Upsilon_{m}
, we notice
(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{H}}_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i})/t
is a square integrable martingale. Thus,
(
Duflo 1997
, Theorem 1.3.15)
suggests that for any
\upsilon>0
,

\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{H}}_{i}-\nabla^{2}f_{i}\right\|=o\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}}{t}}\right).

(E.35)

Combining (
E.34
) and (
E.35
), we obtain
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}\rightarrow\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. For the second result, we suppose
\|\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}\|\leq\Upsilon_{B}^{\star}
and
{\bm{x}}^{T}\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}{\bm{x}}\geq\gamma_{RH}^{\star}\|{\bm{x}}\|^{2}
in the space
\{{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:G^{\star}{\bm{x}}={\bm{0}}\}
. Whenever
\gamma_{RH}<\gamma_{RH}^{\star}
and
\Upsilon_{B}>\Upsilon_{B}^{\star}
, we know
\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}\|\leq\Upsilon_{B}
for large enough
t
. In addition, we let
Z_{t},Z^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(d-m)}
be the matrices whose columns are orthonormal and span the spaces of
\ker(G_{t})
,
\ker(G^{\star})
, respectively. Since
G_{t}\rightarrow G^{\star}
, Davis-Kahan
\sin(\theta)
theorem suggests that
Z_{t}Z_{t}^{T}\rightarrow Z^{\star}(Z^{\star})^{T}
, implying
\inf_{Q}\|Z_{t}-Z^{\star}Q\|\rightarrow 0
with
Q
chosen over all
(d-m)\times(d-m)
orthogonal matrices
(
Davis and Kahan 1970
)
. Thus, we have

\lambda_{\min}(Z_{t}^{T}(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}/t)Z_{t})=\lambda_{\min}(QZ_{t}^{T}(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}/t)Z_{t}Q^{T})\rightarrow\lambda_{\min}((Z^{\star})^{T}\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\star}Z^{\star}),

which implies
\lambda_{\min}(Z_{t}^{T}(\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}/t)Z_{t})\geq\gamma_{RH}
for large enough
t
. This completes the proof.

E.5
Proof of Theorem
5.6

We first improve the rate of
\mathcal{I}_{3,t}
. Lemma
E.5
differs from Lemma
E.3
in the bound of
\bm{\delta}^{t}
. We propose a more precise bound on
\bm{\delta}^{t}
compared to (
E.29
). The new bound relies on the convergence rate of the Hessian
K_{t}
in Lemma
E.6
and Assumption
5.3
for applying Corollary
5.4
.

Lemma E.5
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
5.6
, for any
\upsilon>0
,

\mathcal{I}_{3,t}=o\big(\left\{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})\right\}^{2/3}\{\log(1/\varphi_{t})\}^{\nu}\big)\quad\text{a.s.}

We note that

|\sqrt{1/{\bar{\alpha}}_{t}}-\sqrt{1/\varphi_{t}}|=|\sqrt{{\bar{\alpha}}_{t}}-\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}|/\sqrt{{\bar{\alpha}}_{t}\varphi_{t}}=\left|{\bar{\alpha}}_{t}-\varphi_{t}\right|/(\sqrt{{\bar{\alpha}}_{t}\varphi_{t}}(\sqrt{{\bar{\alpha}}_{t}}+\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}))\leq\chi_{t}/(4\beta_{t}^{1.5}).

Since
\chi<1.5\beta
, we know
\chi_{t}=o(\beta_{t}^{1.5})
. By the almost sure convergence of
({\bm{x}}_{t},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t})
, we only need to show the normality of
1/\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})
. Let us choose
p\in(0,1]
such that

\displaystyle p\chi-0.5p\varphi-2\varphi/3<0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow p>\frac{2\varphi/3}{\chi-0.5\varphi},

(E.36)

\displaystyle(1-\rho^{\tau})+p(\chi-0.5\varphi)/\widetilde{\varphi}>0

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow p<\frac{(1-\rho^{\tau})\widetilde{\varphi}}{0.5\varphi-\chi},

which is guaranteed to exist due to the fact that

0<\frac{2\varphi/3}{\chi-0.5\varphi}<1\wedge\frac{(1-\rho^{\tau})\widetilde{\varphi}}{0.5\varphi-\chi}.

We also choose
q
as stated in the theorem, which guarantees that (by the proof of Theorem
5.5
,
\varphi=\beta
and
\widetilde{\varphi}=\widetilde{\beta}
)

q\chi-q\varphi-0.5\varphi<0\Leftrightarrow q>\frac{0.5\varphi}{\chi-\varphi}\quad\text{and}\quad(1-\rho^{\tau})+q(\chi-\varphi)/\widetilde{\varphi}>0\Leftrightarrow q<\frac{(1-\rho^{\tau})\widetilde{\varphi}}{\varphi-\chi}.

(E.37)

With the above choices of
p
and
q
, we know from (
E.32
), Lemmas
E.2
and
E.5
that for any
\nu>0

\mathcal{I}_{2,t}+\mathcal{I}_{3,t}=O(\chi_{t}^{q}/\varphi_{t}^{q})+o\big(\left\{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})\right\}^{2/3}\left\{\log(1/\varphi_{t})\right\}^{\nu}\big).

The first
O(\cdot)
can be strengthened to
o(\cdot)
when
q<1
. Noting that
1/\sqrt{\varphi_{t}}(\mathcal{I}_{2,t}+\mathcal{I}_{3,t})=o(1)
a.s., the Slutsky’s theorem together with Lemma
E.1
leads to the asymptotic normality. Furthermore, Lemma
B.5
with

A_{t}=\frac{\sqrt{1/\varphi_{t}}\cdot\bm{w}^{T}\mathcal{I}_{1,t}}{\sqrt{\bm{w}^{T}\Xi^{\star}\bm{w}}},\;\;B_{t}=\frac{\sqrt{1/\varphi_{t}}\cdot\bm{w}^{T}(\mathcal{I}_{2,t}+\mathcal{I}_{3,t})}{\sqrt{\bm{w}^{T}\Xi^{\star}\bm{w}}}+\frac{(\sqrt{1/{\bar{\alpha}}_{t}}-\sqrt{1/\varphi_{t}})\cdot\bm{w}^{T}({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})}{\sqrt{\bm{w}^{T}\Xi^{\star}\bm{w}}}

and
C_{t}=0
leads to the Berry-Esseen bound. This completes the proof.

E.5.1
Proof of Lemma
E.5

We need the following lemma to establish the convergence rate of
K_{t}
. The conditions are the same as those for showing the convergence rate of
({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})
, which are weaker than Theorem
5.6
. The proof is provided in Appendix
E.5.2
.

Lemma E.6
.

Under Assumptions
4.1
,
4.2
(
4.2a
,
4.2e
),
4.3
and suppose
\{\beta_{t},\chi_{t}\}_{t}
satisfy (
5.7
). Then, for any
\nu>0
and any constants
p,q\in(0,1]
such that
(1-\rho^{\tau})+p(\chi-0.5\beta)/\widetilde{\beta}>0
and
(1-\rho^{\tau})+q(\chi-\beta)/\widetilde{\beta}>0
, we have

\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|=o\big(\sqrt{\beta_{t}\{\log(1/\beta_{t})\}^{1+\upsilon}}\;\big)+o\big(\chi_{t}^{p}/\beta_{t}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\{\log(1/\chi_{t})\}^{1+\nu}}\;\big)+O(\chi_{t}^{q}/\beta_{t}^{q})\;\;\text{a.s.}

Furthermore, if (
4.2a
) is strengthened to (
4.2b
), then

\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|=O\big(\sqrt{\beta_{t}\log(1/\beta_{t})}\;\big)+O\big(\chi_{t}^{p}/\beta_{t}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\log(1/\chi_{t})}\;\big)+O(\chi_{t}^{q}/\beta_{t}^{q})+o(\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t}\;)\;\text{a.s.}

If
p<1
(and/or
q<1
), the second (and/or third)
O(\cdot)
in the above results can be strengthened to
o(\cdot)
.

Applying Lemma
E.6
with
p,q
chosen to satisfy (
E.36
) and (
E.37
), we know for any
\nu>0

\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|=O\big(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\;\big)+o(\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t}\;).

Combining the above result with (
E.4.3
), Lemmas
5.1
,
E.1
,
E.2
,
E.3
, and Corollary
5.4
, we have

\|\bm{\delta}^{t}\|=O(\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t}))+o(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\cdot\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t}\;).

(E.38)

We plug the above bound into the recursion (
E.27
). We note that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}t\left(1-\frac{\varphi_{t-1}\log(1/\varphi_{t-1})}{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{pequ:12}}}{{=}}\varphi,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}t\left(1-\frac{\sqrt{\varphi_{t-1}\log(1/\varphi_{t-1})}\sqrt{(\log(t-1))^{1+\upsilon}/(t-1)}}{\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t}}\right)=\frac{\varphi}{2}-\frac{1}{2}.

In the following proof, we consider
\varphi
such that
0.5\varphi-0.5\geq\varphi
. Otherwise, the second term in (
E.38
) is absorbed into the first term. Applying Lemma
B.3
and noting that

1.5(1-\rho^{\tau})+(0.5\varphi-0.5)/\widetilde{\varphi}\geq 1.5(1-\rho^{\tau})+\varphi/\widetilde{\varphi}>0,

we know

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3,t}

\displaystyle=o\big(\left\{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})\right\}^{2/3}\big)+o\big(\{\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\cdot\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t}\}^{2/3}\big)

\displaystyle=o\big(\left\{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})\right\}^{2/3}\{\log(1/\varphi_{t})\}^{\nu}\big),

where the second equality is due to
\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t}=O\big(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\{\log(1/\varphi_{t})\}^{1+\nu}}\big)
as implied by the fact that
t\varphi_{t}\rightarrow\widetilde{\varphi}\in(0,\infty]
. This completes the proof.

E.5.2
Proof of Lemma
E.6

We note from the proof of Theorem
5.5
that
\varphi=\beta
and
\widetilde{\varphi}=\widetilde{\beta}
. By Lemmas
5.1
,
E.1
,
E.2
,
E.3
, for any
\nu>0
, if (
4.2a
) holds, then we have

\|({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})\|=o\big(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\{\log(1/\varphi_{t})\}^{1+\nu}}\big)+o\big(\chi_{t}^{p}/\varphi_{t}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\{\log(1/\chi_{t})\}^{1+\nu}}\;\big)+O(\chi_{t}^{q}/\varphi_{t}^{q}).

(E.39)

Here,
p,q\in(0,1]
are any constants such that
(1-\rho^{\tau})+p(\chi-0.5\varphi)/\widetilde{\varphi}>0
and
(1-\rho^{\tau})+q(\chi-\varphi)/\widetilde{\varphi}>0
. Furthermore, if
q<1
the
O(\cdot)
in the third term can be strengthened to
o(\cdot)
. In the following proof, we only consider
p,q
such that

p(\chi-0.5\varphi)\geq 0.5\varphi\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad q(\chi-\varphi)\geq 0.5\varphi.

(E.40)

Otherwise, by (
E.31
), (
E.32
), Lemmas
B.1
and
B.2
, we know the second (and/or third) term in (
E.39
) can be absorbed into the first term. We now apply (
4.1
), combine (
E.34
) and (
E.35
), and have for any
\upsilon>0
and large enough
t
that

\displaystyle\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|\leq\frac{\Upsilon_{L}}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|+\Upsilon_{L}\|{\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\|+o\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}}{t}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Upsilon_{L}}{t}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{0}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{0}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|+\Upsilon_{L}\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left(1-\frac{1}{j}\right)\frac{1}{i}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|+\Upsilon_{L}\|{\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\|+o\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}}{t}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Upsilon_{L}\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left(1-\frac{1}{j}\right)\frac{1}{i}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|+\Upsilon_{L}\|{\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\|+o\left(\sqrt{\frac{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}}{t}}\right).

(E.41)

We claim that
\varphi=\beta>-2
. Otherwise,
\varphi+1.5\leq-0.5<0
. We apply Lemma
B.1
and have

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}t\left(1-\frac{\varphi_{t-1}(t-1)^{1.5}}{\varphi_{t}t^{1.5}}\right)=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}t\left(1-\frac{\varphi_{t-1}}{\varphi_{t}}+\frac{\varphi_{t-1}}{\varphi_{t}}\left(1-\frac{(t-1)^{1.5}}{t^{1.5}}\right)\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{cond:varphi}}}{{=}}\varphi+1.5<0.

Then, Lemma
B.2
suggests
\varphi_{t}t^{1.5}\rightarrow 0
, which cannot hold under (
5.7
). Thus,
\varphi>-2
. Using (
E.31
), (
E.32
), (
E.40
), and Lemma
B.3
, and noting that
1+p(\chi-0.5\varphi)\geq 1+0.5\varphi>0
and
1+q(\chi-\varphi)\geq 1+0.5\varphi>0
, we obtain

\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left(1-\frac{1}{j}\right)\frac{1}{i}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|\\
=o\big(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\{\log(1/\varphi_{t})\}^{1+\nu}}\big)+o\big(\chi_{t}^{p}/\varphi_{t}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\{\log(1/\chi_{t})\}^{1+\nu}}\;\big)+O(\chi_{t}^{q}/\varphi_{t}^{q}).

(E.42)

Following the same derivation, we know that if (
4.2b
) holds, then

\|({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})\|=O\big(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\big)+O\big(\chi_{t}^{p}/\varphi_{t}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\log(1/\chi_{t})}\;\big)+O(\chi_{t}^{q}/\varphi_{t}^{q})

(E.43)

and

\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\left(1-\frac{1}{j}\right)\frac{1}{i}\left\|\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}^{\star}\\
{\bm{\lambda}}_{i}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star}\end{pmatrix}\right\|\\
=O\big(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\big)+O\big(\chi_{t}^{p}/\varphi_{t}^{0.5p}\sqrt{\log(1/\chi_{t})}\;\big)+O(\chi_{t}^{q}/\varphi_{t}^{q}).

(E.44)

Combining (
E.5.2
), (
E.42
), (
E.44
) together, and noting that
\beta_{t}\leq\varphi_{t}\leq 2\beta_{t}
and
o(\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t})
can be absorbed into
o\big(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\{\log(1/\varphi_{t})\}^{1+\nu}}\big)
under (
4.2a
) (as implied by the fact that
t\varphi_{t}\rightarrow\widetilde{\varphi}\in(0,\infty]
), we complete the proof.

E.6
Proof of Corollary
5.7

The result in (a) is immediate by plugging
\beta=-1
and
\widetilde{\beta}=1
into (
5.12
). For (b), we plug
\beta=-1
and
\widetilde{\beta}=1
into (
5.9
), and know that
{\Xi^{\star}}
solves the equation

(0.5I+C^{\star}){\Xi^{\star}}+{\Xi^{\star}}(0.5I+C^{\star})=\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}].

Thus, we have

(0.5I+C^{\star})({\Xi^{\star}}-{\Omega^{\star}})+({\Xi^{\star}}-{\Omega^{\star}})(0.5I+C^{\star})\\
=\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]-{\Omega^{\star}}-C^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}-{\Omega^{\star}}C^{\star}=\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\succeq{\bm{0}}.

Since
\|C^{\star}\|\leq\rho^{\tau}<0.5
, the basic Lyapunov theorem (cf.
(
Khalil 2002
, Theorem 4.6)
) suggests that
{\Xi^{\star}}\succeq{\Omega^{\star}}
. Furthermore, with the notation in (
5.11
), we know

{\Xi^{\star}}-{\Omega^{\star}}=U\big(\Theta\circ U^{T}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\;U\big)U^{T}\quad\text{ with }\quad[\Theta]_{k,l}=1/(\sigma_{k}+\sigma_{l}-1).

(E.45)

The matrix
\Theta
is positive semidefinite since for any vector
\bm{\omega}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\omega}^{T}\Theta\bm{\omega}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,l=1}^{d+m}\frac{\bm{\omega}_{k}\bm{\omega}_{l}}{\sigma_{k}+\sigma_{l}-1}=\sum_{k,l=1}^{d+m}\bm{\omega}_{k}\bm{\omega}_{l}\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp(-s(\sigma_{k}+\sigma_{l}-1))ds\quad(\text{since }\sigma_{k}+\sigma_{l}-1>0)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{d+m}\bm{\omega}_{k}\exp(-s(\sigma_{k}-0.5))\right)^{2}ds\geq 0.

By
(
Horn and Johnson 1985
, 7.5.P24)
, we have

\|{\Xi^{\star}}-{\Omega^{\star}}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{nequ:8}}}{{=}}\|\Theta\circ U^{T}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\;U\|\\
\leq\max_{k}[\Theta]_{k,k}\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\|\leq\frac{1}{1-2\rho^{\tau}}\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\|\leq 3\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\|,

where the second last inequality is due to
\sigma_{k}\geq 1-\rho^{\tau}
and the last inequality is due to
\rho^{\tau}<1/3
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{0}}

\displaystyle\preceq\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\preceq\|{\Omega^{\star}}\|\cdot\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]

\displaystyle=\|{\Omega^{\star}}\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\prod_{j=1}^{\tau}(I-K^{\star}S_{j}(S_{j}^{T}(K^{\star})S)^{\dagger}S_{j}^{T}K^{\star})\right\}\left\{\prod_{j=1}^{\tau}(I-K^{\star}S_{j}(S_{j}^{T}(K^{\star})S)^{\dagger}S_{j}^{T}K^{\star})\right\}^{T}\right]

\displaystyle=\|{\Omega^{\star}}\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left\{\prod_{j=2}^{\tau}(I-K^{\star}S_{j}(S_{j}^{T}(K^{\star})S_{j})^{\dagger}S_{j}^{T}K^{\star})\right\}\mathbb{E}[(I-K^{\star}S_{1}(S_{1}^{T}(K^{\star})S_{1})^{\dagger}S_{j}^{T}K^{\star})\mid S_{2:\tau}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left\{\prod_{j=2}^{\tau}(I-K^{\star}S_{j}(S_{j}^{T}(K^{\star})S_{j})^{\dagger}S_{j}^{T}K^{\star})\right\}^{T}\bigg]

\displaystyle\preceq\rho\|{\Omega^{\star}}\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\prod_{j=2}^{\tau}(I-K^{\star}S_{j}(S_{j}^{T}(K^{\star})S)^{\dagger}S_{j}^{T}K^{\star})\right\}\left\{\prod_{j=2}^{\tau}(I-K^{\star}S_{j}(S_{j}^{T}(K^{\star})S)^{\dagger}S_{j}^{T}K^{\star})\right\}^{T}\right]

\displaystyle\preceq\rho^{\tau}\|{\Omega^{\star}}\|\cdot I,

(E.46)

where the second last inequality is from Assumption
4.3
and
K_{t}\rightarrow K^{\star}
; and the last inequality applies the same reason for sketch matrices
S_{2:\tau}
. Combining the above two displays completes the proof.

E.7
Proof of Theorem
5.10

We have

\left\|\Xi_{t}-\Xi^{\star}\right\|\leq\|\Xi^{\star}-\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})\|\\
+\|\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]-{\Omega^{\star}}\|/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})+\left\|{\Omega^{\star}}-\Omega_{t}\right\|/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta}).

(E.47)

For the first term in (
E.47
), we have
(
Horn and Johnson 1985
, 7.7.P27)

\displaystyle\|\Xi^{\star}

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})\|

\displaystyle\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{equ:Xi:n}}}}{{=}}\;\|(\Theta-{\bm{1}}{\bm{1}}^{T}/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta}))\circ U^{T}\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]U\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Theta-{\bm{1}}{\bm{1}}^{T}/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})\|\cdot\|\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\|\quad(\|A\circ B\|\leq\|A\|\cdot\|B\|)

\displaystyle\leq 4\|\Theta-{\bm{1}}{\bm{1}}^{T}/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})\|\cdot\|{\Omega^{\star}}\|\quad(\|\widetilde{C}^{\star}\|\leq 1),

and for any
1\leq k,l\leq d+m
,

|\Theta_{k,l}-1/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})|=|1/(\sigma_{k}+\sigma_{l}+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})-1/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})|=\frac{\left|2-\sigma_{k}-\sigma_{l}\right|}{(\sigma_{k}+\sigma_{l}+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})}\\
\leq\frac{2\rho^{\tau}}{(2-2\rho^{\tau}+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\eqref{cond:n:1}}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2\rho^{\tau}}{(2-2(1+\beta/(1.5\widetilde{\beta}))+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})}=\frac{6\rho^{\tau}}{-\beta/\widetilde{\beta}(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})}.

Therefore, the above two displays lead to

\|\Xi^{\star}-\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]/(2+\beta/\widetilde{\beta})\|=O(\rho^{\tau}).

(E.48)

For the second term in (
E.47
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star}){\Omega^{\star}}(I+\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]-{\Omega^{\star}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|C^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}\|+\|{\Omega^{\star}}C^{\star}\|+\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\|\quad(\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}]=C^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq 2\|{\Omega^{\star}}\|\rho^{\tau}+\|\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{C}^{\star}{\Omega^{\star}}(\widetilde{C}^{\star})^{T}]\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{nequ:9}}}{{=}}O(\rho^{\tau}).

(E.49)

For the third term in (
E.47
), we have

\|\Omega_{t}-{\Omega^{\star}}\|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{equ:Omega}}}{{=}}O(\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|)\\
+O\left(\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T}-\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}\right)\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}\right)^{T}-\mathbb{E}[\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)]+\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star})\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star})\right\|\right).

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T}-\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}\right)\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}\right)^{T}-\mathbb{E}[\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)]+\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star})\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T}-\mathbb{E}[\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)]\right\|+\left\|\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}\right)\left(\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}\right)^{T}-\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star})\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star})\right\|.

We take the first term as an example, while the second term has the same guarantee following the same derivations. We note that

\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T}-\mathbb{E}[\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)]\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}({\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T})-\mathbb{E}[{\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right\|\\
+\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}[{\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)]\right\|.

By (
4.2c
), we know the first term on the right hand side is a square integrable martingale. The strong law of large number
(
Duflo 1997
, Theorem 1.3.15)
suggests that for any
\nu>0

\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}({\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T})-\mathbb{E}[{\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]\right\|=o\big(\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t}\big).

By (
E.12
), (
E.44
), and the choices of
p,q
in (
E.36
) and (
E.37
), the second term on the right hand side can be bounded by

\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}[{\bar{g}}_{i}{\bar{g}}_{i}^{T}\mid\mathcal{F}_{i-1}]-\mathbb{E}[\nabla f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)\nabla^{T}f({\bm{x}}^{\star};\xi)]\right\|=O\left(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\right).

(E.50)

Combining the above five displays with Lemma
E.6
, we have

\|\Omega_{t}-{\Omega^{\star}}\|=O\left(\sqrt{\varphi_{t}\log(1/\varphi_{t})}\right)+o\big(\sqrt{(\log t)^{1+\upsilon}/t}\big).

(E.51)

Combining (
E.47
), (
E.48
), (
E.7
), and (
E.51
), we complete the first part of the proof. For the Berry-Esseen inequality, we simply note that

\frac{\bm{w}^{T}({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})}{\sqrt{\bm{w}^{T}\Xi_{t}\bm{w}}}=\frac{\bm{w}^{T}({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})}{\sqrt{\bm{w}^{T}\Xi^{\star}\bm{w}}\cdot\sqrt{1+\frac{\bm{w}^{T}\Xi_{t}\bm{w}-\bm{w}^{T}\Xi^{\star}\bm{w}}{\bm{w}^{T}\Xi^{\star}\bm{w}}}}.

Thus, we apply Theorem
5.6
and Lemma
B.5
, and complete the proof.

Appendix F
Additional Experimental Results

In this section, we provide more implementation details and show additional results. We follow the introduction in Section
6
, and implement the method on eight problems in CUTEst test set and on linearly/nonlinearly constrained regression problems. For both implementation, we run
10^{5}
iterations, set
\beta_{t}=1/t^{0.501}
,
\chi_{t}=\beta_{t}^{2}
, and
{\bar{\alpha}}_{t}\sim\text{Uniform}([\beta_{t},\eta_{t}])
with
\eta_{t}=\beta_{t}+\chi_{t}
. Regarding the Hessian regularization
\Delta_{t}
, we let (
\lambda_{\min}(\cdot)
denotes the least eigenvalue)

\Delta_{t}\coloneqq(-\lambda_{\min}(Z_{t}^{T}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}Z_{t})/t+0.1)\cdot I\quad\text{whenever}\quad\lambda_{\min}(Z_{t}^{T}\sum_{i=0}^{t-1}{\bar{\nabla}}_{{\bm{x}}}^{2}\mathcal{L}_{i}Z_{t})<0.

Here,
Z_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(d-m)}
has orthonormal columns that span the space
\{{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:G_{t}{\bm{x}}={\bm{0}}\}
, which is obtained from the QR decomposition.

F.1
CUTEst problems

In this section, we testify the convergence rate in Theorem
5.5
. In particular, we randomly pick one run across 200 runs, and show the convergence plots of the KKT residual
\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{t}\|
, the mean absolute error
\|({\bm{x}}_{t}-{\bm{x}}^{\star},{\bm{\lambda}}_{t}-{\bm{\lambda}}^{\star})\|
, and the Hessian error
\|K_{t}-K^{\star}\|
. By Theorem
5.5
, Lemma
E.6
, and the Lipschitz continuity of the Hessian, the theoretical convergence rate for these three quantities is
O(\sqrt{\beta_{t}\log(1/\beta_{t})})
.

The convergence plots are shown in Figures
1
and
2
. We use six problems for illustration.
From the figures, we observe that the method converges faster for small sampling variance
\sigma^{2}
and converges slower for large
\sigma^{2}
. Specifically, our theoretical convergence rate precisely characterizes asymptotic behavior of the method, and
\sigma^{2}
only affects the rate as a constant factor.

, which is the theoretical asymptotic rate.

F.2
Constrained regression problems

We follow the experiments in Section
6.2
, and provide comprehensive comparisons between inexact and exact second-order methods on linearly/nonlinearly constrained regression problems. The coefficient matrix
A
in linear constraints is sampled from standard normal; the objective of logistic loss is regularized by a quadratic penalty term with a unit
parameter
. We vary the parameters

d,r
and
\tau
. In particular, we let
d\in\{5,20,40,60\}
,
r\in\{0.4,0.5,0.6\}
for Toeplitz
\Sigma_{a}
and
r\in\{0.1,0.2,0.3\}
for Equi-correlation
\Sigma_{a}
, and
\tau\in\{\infty,20,40,60\}
. We
mention
that
\tau=\infty
corresponds to the exact method. For each setup, we perform 200 independent runs.

The extensive comparison results of offline
M
-estimation and StoSQP with different
\tau
are reported in Tables
4
-
11
. Specifically, Tables
4
and
5
summarize the results of linear model + linear constraints; Tables
6
and
7
summarize the results of linear model + nonlinear constraints; Tables
8
and
9
summarize the results of logistic model +
linear
constraints; while Tables
10
and
11
summarize the results of logistic model + nonlinear constraints. For all four cases, we have the following observations.

For MAE, we observe that
M
-estimation achieves results that are an order of magnitude smaller than those of the StoSQP methods. Among the different setups of
\tau
of StoSQP, we find that exact StoSQP
(\tau=\infty)
generally yields smaller MAE compared to
inexact
StoSQP. Furthermore, a larger
\tau
(i.e., more sketching steps) in StoSQP tends to result in smaller MAE, although the differences are less evident than those observed between StoSQP and
M
-estimation methods. This trend is robust across different setups of the design covariance
\Sigma_{a}
. For instance, for
d=40
in Table
5
, StoSQP with
\tau=20
achieves an MAE of approximately 0.2–0.25, while StoSQP with
\tau=40,60,\infty
achieves an MAE of less than 0.1. This observation suggests that, given problem parameters such as the condition number of the Lagrangian Hessian and problem dimension,
\tau=20
may be
insufficient
for
solving

Newton

systems
in this
scenario
. Specifically, solving exact Newton systems requires
O(46^{3})=O(97,336)
flops while a
sketching
solver with
\tau=20
requires only
O(20\times 46)=O(920)
flops. This substantial reduction in computational cost can result in insufficient precision for the Newton direction at each step, leading to a larger MAE when the sample size is fixed.

For coverage rate, we observe that StoSQP with different
\tau
generally achieves a valid coverage rate that is very close to the nominal rate 95%, matching the performance of offline
M
-estimation. There are two potential exceptions.
The first exception occurs when
d=5
, where
StoSQP exhibits undercoverage with rates ranging from 87%-92% (cf.
Tables

4
,
8
, and
10
). This undercoverage occurs because the condition numbers of the Lagrangian Hessian in these problems are significantly larger than in other scenarios, despite the small problem dimension of
5
. Consequently, the iteration sequence of StoSQP may not have reached stationarity given the limited sample size. As noted in
Zhu et al. 2021
, even SGD-based estimators require
3\times 10^{5}
to
4\times 10^{5}
samples to alleviate undercoverage issue in challenging online inference tasks for unconstrained problems, while we only have
10^{5}
samples for constrained
problems
. Furthermore, applying a sketching solver to such small-scale problems seems an overkill. We recommend using a simple linear system solver for small-scale problems to reduce the additional uncertainty introduced by the sketching solver.
The second exception occurs when
d=40
, where StoSQP with
\tau=20
exhibits undercoverage with rates ranging from 82%-86%. In contrast, StoSQP with
\tau=40,60,\infty
achieves valid coverage in this case (cf. Table
5
). As
explained
for MAE, this undercoverage results from insufficient sketching steps, which not only make the StoSQP iteration sequence noisy but also make the bias of covariance matrix estimation non-negligible.
For this scenario, slightly increasing the number of sketching steps (e.g., setting
\tau=40
) can resolve the undercoverage issue while still preserving computational efficiency compared to exact methods.

For average length of confidence intervals, we observe that
M
-estimation produces intervals that are an order of magnitude shorter than those of the StoSQP methods. Among the StoSQP methods, the inexact settings
(\tau<\infty)
yield average lengths very similar to the exact setting
(\tau=\infty)
, indicating that the inexact methods do not lead to overly conservative intervals. For both Toeplitz and Equi-correlation
\Sigma_{a}
, the length of the confidence intervals remains largely unchanged across different setups of
r
. Moreover, for both linear and logistic models, nonlinear constraints tend to result in wider confidence intervals for both offline and online methods, as shown by comparisons of Tables
4
,
5
with Tables
6
,
7
and Tables
8
,
9
with Tables
10
,
11
.

For computational flops per iteration, we observe that online StoSQP methods are significantly more efficient than the offline method. A sketching solver can further reduce the computational costs of StoSQP. Choosing the sketching step
\tau
involves a trade-off between computational and statistical efficiency. As shown in Table
5
, when
d=40
,
\tau=20
requires fewer flops but achieves larger MAE and lower coverage rates, whereas
\tau=60
requires more flops (though still fewer than those of
M
-estimation and
\tau=\infty
) but achieves lower MAE and better coverage rates. In this case,
\tau=40
strikes a better balance between the two aspects. We should also mention that for small-scale problems
(d=5)
, using a sketching solver with a large
\tau
is counterproductive, as it may cause the flops to exceed those of the exact solver.
